Runtime mode check (2026-11-05): the SPA, API, Horizon, mail worker, scheduler, Nginx, Reverb, and Redis services use the local dev Compose project; API and Reverb source mounts are active. Delta 898 stopped another 12-container `ogami-erp-e2e` respawn and its unique runner process group, preserving E2E volumes. The ten dev containers remain running; the dev database was not stopped or seeded. No E2E result is counted. The external launcher is unidentified and may respawn the isolated project.

During Delta 798, both `node scripts/e2e/runner.mjs seed` and then `node scripts/e2e/runner.mjs up` reappeared under the managed Codex app-server, along with 12 `ogami-erp-e2e` containers. Each runner/Compose child was terminated; restart was disabled and only those labeled containers were stopped, with volumes preserved. Final check found no test runner or E2E container active; the ten local development services remained running and `ogamiph.dev` plus `/@vite/client` returned HTTP 200. The external managed process may relaunch them again.

## Current execution cursor — Delta 986 (2026-11-05 UTC)

### Delta 985 — close a live route inventory miss and tighten producer-call evidence

The runtime route contract found `PATCH api/v1/mrp/scheduler/{schedule}/move` in Laravel but absent from both the endpoint inventory and stale-risk matrix. Traced it to `SchedulerController::move` → locked `CapacityPlanningService::move`, which revalidates `expected_updated_at` and writes schedule times through Eloquent; the registered `ProductionScheduleRealtimeObserver` stages the existing after-commit `ProductionScheduleChanged` outbox signal. Added the route and source-only evidence to both CSVs. All UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; the approval inbox is `NOT_APPLICABLE`. Route/matrix counts are now 758 (681 HTTP mutations, five state-changing GETs, 71 scheduled invocations, one model-create writer); transitions remain 596 with 566 `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The six-row `direct-service:` contract previously treated `Producer::class` as sufficient evidence. A red-first focused regression failed on a class constant without a method call; the guard now requires an invoked service method from `app()`/`resolve()` or a static method call, while still following same-class helper calls. `RealtimeMutationCoverageContractTest.php` passes all 6 tests / 4,301 assertions; PHPUnit reports 6 container warnings. The first run exposed the missing scheduler route; after inventory reconciliation the focused contract passes. The API container's mounted `/var/docs/realtime` directory was empty, so the final check used a temporary `/tmp/realtime-contract` copy of the current docs via `REALTIME_REPO_ROOT`; that temporary copy was removed after verification. No production route mutation, browser/two-user scenario, business data, Redis key or dev service was changed. This proves the checked route inventory and six direct service paths only; peer UI delivery and the remaining 186 producer paths remain unverified.


### Delta 986 — reconcile the scheduler move on the acting user's page (2026-11-05 UTC)

A focused mocked-API regression reproduced the actor-side stale interval: after `PATCH /mrp/scheduler/{schedule}/move` returned new schedule times, the cached Gantt bar remained at its old position while the snapshot refetch was pending. The response reconciliation found the matching bar, but the row updater used unchanged array length as its change test and returned the original row, discarding the replacement bar. It now tracks whether the row contained the moved schedule and retains the updated row. The selected regression passes and proves the new date renders before the refetch settles; changed-file ESLint passes. Running the entire pre-existing schedule test file resulted in 3 passed / 4 failed tests (multiple existing query/DOM expectations); this has not been compared against a baseline and is not counted as regression-free. ### Delta 983 — remove terminal Payroll progress snapshots (2026-11-05 UTC)

`PayrollPeriodResource` returns `compute_progress` only while status is `Processing`, but `ProcessPayrollJob` emitted its terminal progress through the same tracker as active progress. A red-first focused `PayrollComputeClaimFencingTest` run completed the job and found the terminal `0/0` snapshot still present in the 3600-second cache (1 test failed, 4 assertions). The tracker now writes only while the model is Processing; the shared `releaseClaim()` path schedules snapshot deletion after commit, covering job success/failure, force-unlock and the stale reaper. The terminal refresh event remains broadcast for clients and carries no progress values. The same focused test now passes (1 warning, 4 assertions; 21.55 seconds; known API test-container warning). PHP syntax checks pass; Pint first found two style issues, then fixed them in the four touched PHP files. No broad suite ran. An unrelated full API suite had been active in the shared `ogami-api` container for 28 minutes and held the test DB during the first green attempt; following the user's prior instruction to stop broad testing, its exact process group was terminated. The test DB then showed no active sessions and the isolated regression passed. No dev business data or dev service was changed. Runtime cache contents/hit rate and UI latency were not measured.

### Delta 982 — reconcile persistent retry state against a cold-start auth scope (2026-11-05 UTC)

Cold-start auth bootstrap had no persisted prior identity/scope to compare, so local/session retry payloads could survive a same-user permission change while the SPA was closed; first-upgrade browsers without a marker also retained old retries. Red-first auth-store checks reproduced both cases. `applyUser()` now reconciles a small `ogami:auth-cache-scope:v1` marker containing the user ID, opaque access fingerprint, and write time. A changed, malformed, missing-with-app-state, or expired (30-day) marker clears app-owned drafts/retries before the new scope is published. Same-scope retries remain available across cold bootstrap; logout clears the marker. A related form-draft test expectation was corrected to assert that access-scope changes remove the retry payload too, while the legacy sweeper still preserves differently shaped journals absent an auth-scope change. Focused auth-store tests pass 19/19 and form-draft tests pass 6/6; targeted ESLint passes. This is browser-storage unit evidence only: no real browser role change, multiple-user terminal, tablet, peer WebSocket, transition-matrix cell, or latency claim is added. No API/database data or dev service was changed.

A consistency pass also found the Payroll compute-claim matrix note still described a missing list ping after the service had already added an explicit in-transaction generation rotation and `PayrollPeriodChanged` outbox signal. Corrected the source note to match the current code and existing focused claim-outbox test; all corresponding UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` because there is no two-user render evidence.

### Delta 981 — isolate persisted form drafts by current authorization scope (2026-11-05 UTC)

Reproduced a shared-terminal cache gap: drafts used only `userId:formKey`; after a full reload, the auth store had no prior role to compare and retained the same user's earlier-scope draft. The red-first hook regression observed the saved payload remained after resolving the same user ID with a different role. A second red-first regression showed the startup sweeper left a valid, unexpired legacy draft in the old namespace. Ordinary drafts now use a v2 key scoped by user, role, employee/department, active state, permissions, delegations, and features; the hook writes/reads only when the supplied ID matches the active auth identity. Initial `applyUser()` prunes that user's obsolete/legacy drafts, and the legacy sweep removes recognized autosave envelopes while preserving differently shaped pending-operation journals. Updated the ChecklistDecisionPanel fixture to use the scoped key. The focused hook file passes 6/6 and the focused ChecklistDecisionPanel draft-restore case passes (1 selected, 24 skipped); targeted ESLint passes. The two new behavior tests were each observed failing before the corresponding cleanup/key-scope behavior was restored. No browser role-switch, shop-floor tablet, broader SPA suite, matrix promotion, or latency measurement ran. No business data changed; PWA/local dev runtime remains active.

### Delta 978 — guard wait monitoring for every Horizon lane (2026-11-05 UTC)

Expanded the queue config regression to iterate every default Horizon supervisor and each served `<connection>:<queue>` pair, requiring an explicit positive wait threshold. This would have caught Delta 977's `redis_broadcasts:high` mismatch and also prevents a future supervisor from silently serving an unmonitored lane. The focused test passes with 15 assertions in 0.34 seconds; PHPUnit emits the existing API-container warning about missing `/var/www/.env`. No product runtime, queued data, cache, or business record was changed in this delta. The live configuration fix and Horizon restart are documented in Delta 977.

### Delta 977 — make Horizon monitor the active outbox queue (2026-11-05 UTC)

Source tracing Horizon's installed `MonitorWaitTimes` confirmed its lookup key is `<connection>:<queue>`. The active outbox supervisor uses `redis_broadcasts:high`, but config only had a `redis:high` threshold, so its 15-second long-wait alert could not trigger. Added `redis_broadcasts:high => 15` and a focused queue-config regression. The red-first case failed because the configured value was null; after the fix it passes (1 assertion, 0.34 seconds, with the container's existing missing-`.env` warning). - Delta 680 adds optimistic concurrency to Overtime status transitions. A red-first API regression showed that a stale `If-Match: "0"` still allowed the owner to cancel a pending request. `overtime_requests.version` is now a monotonic integer initialized/backfilled to 1; API resources expose it, the private refresh event includes only action/version/timestamp metadata, and approve/reject/cancel/restore plus bulk approve compare supplied versions after locking the current row. Stale versions use the existing typed 409 response; callers that omit the optional header keep the existing endpoint behavior. SPA decision and owner actions send the current version, and bulk approval sends a version map. The dev migration was applied and the five existing overtime rows all have version 1. Focused stale/current cancel tests pass (2 tests / 8 assertions), bulk stale-version test passes (1 / 4), event payload test passes (1 / 1); targeted SPA ESLint and project typecheck pass, as do PHP syntax checks. ## Historical execution cursor — delta 630

Latest cache/runtime finding: Laravel's `horizon:terminate` writes `illuminate:queue:restart` forever, bypassing the earlier queue:restart-only TTL listener. The live cache audit found this exact key as its only TTL-less entry in DB 3, where `allkeys-lru` could evict it and the scheduled prune could delete it. Delta 622 makes this one framework key an explicit persistent exception, covers both restart commands, protects it from prune and Redis eviction with `volatile-lru`, and records that no stale business record or lost restart was reproduced. Focused cache/restart tests pass 7 cases / 34 assertions; Compose config validates; live dev Redis now reports `volatile-lru`, audit reports 0 unbounded application entries plus the 1 expected framework marker, and prune apply found/deleted 0 keys. The Redis cache container was recreated to persist the policy, clearing its disposable payload keyspace; no database/business data changed, but temporary cold-cache impact was not measured. This did not measure cache hit rate, API p95, forced eviction behavior, production Redis, or multi-user UI delivery.

The call-site appendix now has 432 rows (136 application, 239 test, 57 database/migration): a focused executable-source scan found no missing direct `Cache::...` facade/helper operations, and delta 623 added the two injected-Repository operations used to preserve the queue restart marker. This is inventory coverage only; the complete cache registry's semantic invalidation evidence and hit-rate/API latency measurements remain incomplete.

The latest source-ledger pass reconciled three User Invite rows, seven Attendance shift/assignment rows, and nine Inventory/CRM/Production rows whose evidence still described pre-fix missing producers despite current event/channel/subscriber coverage. ## Delta 536 — preserve Purchase Request create-and-submit response (2026-11-03 UTC)

The combined Purchase Request create flow called `create()` and then `submit()`, but discarded the submitted resource and navigated with no detail-cache entry. The first approval-board browser run reproduced the actor detail failing to load after create/submit: POST returned 201, submit PATCH returned 200, and three detail GETs were aborted by the browser (Nginx 499). Source inspection confirmed the final API response was not used. A focused SPA regression first failed because the detail query cache was undefined after the combined mutation.

The create mutation now returns the submit response and calls the shared `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` before navigation, installing that authoritative record under its exact detail key and invalidating mapped related families. The regression passes 1/1, targeted ESLint passes, and the SPA TypeScript check passes. A second headless two-context run on `ogamiph.dev` passed end to end: the Finance actor socket was forcibly closed; the actor's Approve action disappeared 89 ms after PATCH 200; the VP inbox received a private refresh signal and rendered the request under My action 892 ms after the actor response. This is one live sample, not a p95. It validates this combined create/submit path and Finance→VP inbox behavior only; other Purchase Request routes/surfaces remain per-matrix. The initial 499s confirm browser-side aborts, but the precise cancellation trigger was not isolated. A narrow source sweep found the Sales Order create→confirm flow already consumes the confirm response and reconciles it through the same helper; no other direct create-then-transition-and-discard sequence matched that scan. The scenario created and advanced one test Purchase Request. No broad suites or cache performance measurements were run.

## Confirmed source/config findings and work already in the tree

The detailed findings are in `01-root-causes.md`, `00-discovery.md`, and `cache-registry.md`. Broad themes include missing or narrow event paths, query-builder/bulk writers bypassing model observers, cross-record refresh gaps, client query families that did not share invalidation, and backend/client cache namespaces whose invalidation and restore behavior needed explicit ownership. Infrastructure/channel and runtime findings are recorded with their evidence and limitations. Never upgrade a source candidate to a reproduced user-visible bug without the corresponding run.

The working tree already contains foundation changes across the API, SPA, Compose/configuration and documentation: durable after-commit outbox broadcasts and queue separation, private refresh-only channels, shared Echo/query invalidation hooks, active-query catch-up and identity cache clearing, HTTP/PWA cache rules, scoped cache-generation/invalidation work, Redis/cache hygiene tooling, deployment/config notes, and many module-specific observers, hooks, action maps, and related-record refreshes. Database restore and rollback already rotate the registered data-snapshot cache generations before service resume, then send private ERP/customer-portal/supplier-portal reload pings; runtime verification remains outstanding. The recent module work includes Payroll, MRP-to-SalesOrder, Attendance/Overtime/Shift, Loans, Return Management, Inventory/Warehouse, Quality, Maintenance and other areas. Detailed source/status notes live in the matrix, event catalog, payload audit, cache registry and root-cause report.

## Current slice — delta 314: HR shift and employee-assignment refreshes

Source tracing found that remote HR shift lists, assignment lists, the employee editor’s current-shift query, and self-service shift summaries had no direct Shift/EmployeeShiftAssignment event family. Two quiet write paths were also present: `ShiftService` flips other default shifts with query-builder updates, and `EmployeeService` seeds an initial assignment using `insertOrIgnore`.

Added:

- `ShiftChanged` and `ShiftAssignmentChanged`, content-free and dispatched after commit on the dedicated broadcast queue via the durable outbox.
- `ShiftRealtimeObserver` and `ShiftAssignmentRealtimeObserver`, registered in `AppServiceProvider`.
- An explicit Shift service bridge for query-builder default flips and an EmployeeService bridge that stages only when `insertOrIgnore` inserts a row.
- Private channels for Shift records, HR shift/assignment lists, linked active employees’ shift refreshes, and exact employee assignment refreshes. Channel signals carry no shift or employee business fields.
- A nullable `updated_at` column, backfilled from `created_at` where available, for assignment version metadata; the model now maintains timestamps.
- Central SPA invalidation entries and subscriptions on the HR shift list, assignment screen, employee edit page and self-service layout.
- Source-specific notes for the seven ShiftController mutation rows and EmployeeController create row. Their actor/peer/list/action/related/badge/dashboard/inbox outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Bulk assignment currently stages a row-scoped outbox ping for each changed assignment. Queue burst impact is not measured; evaluate transaction/entity coalescing before expanding this pattern. No business status or shift-assignment rules were changed.

## Current slice — delta 318: Production access to live Sales Order fulfillment previews

Source review corrected root cause #191: the fulfillment controller already invokes `MrpEngineService::runForSalesOrder`; an actual policy change alters the planning fingerprint, so the MRP path stages a parent SalesOrder refresh before commit. The remaining gap was audience and query coverage. The fulfillment preview API permits Production confirmers without `crm.sales_orders.view`, but the existing SalesOrder channels were CRM-scoped and the dialog had no event listener or central preview-query mapping.

Added:

- An exact private `sales-order-fulfillment.{hashId}` channel with the same active-user, CRM feature, Sales Order view or Production confirm permissions, and non-deleted order scope as the preview route. `ChainStepAdvanced` sends the existing refresh-only event to it.
- A live listener in `FulfillmentDialog` that invalidates the exact preview through the central `sales_order` map. The map also targets the exact fulfillment query when a normal SalesOrder event is received.
- Immediate actor reconciliation from the fulfillment mutation response and targeted invalidation of linked SalesOrder views instead of invalidating the entire QueryClient.
- A conflict review gate that preserves local line choices and the planning reason on peer changes, then requires an explicit preview refresh before confirmation. A stale-revision API error triggers the same review state without changing the endpoint's status code or business rule.

## Current slice — delta 319: Inbound Shipment aggregate refresh and stale-write handling

Source trace confirmed that Shipment, Container, ShipmentDocument and ShipmentLandedCost writes had no shared refresh signal or SPA entity-map/subscription. Shipment Received could signal a separate GRN but did not refresh Shipment readers. Existing dashboard and badge model observers already retire their registered generations for Shipment lifecycle writes; this slice adds no backend cache family.

Added:

- `ShipmentChanged` through the transaction-coalesced durable outbox and dedicated after-commit broadcasts queue. Shipment lifecycle plus container/document/landed-cost observers converge on the parent Shipment; landed-cost query-builder deletes remain covered by the parent save in the same transaction.
- Private exact Shipment and entity-list channels whose authorization matches the feature and read permissions on Shipment API routes. The payload contains only opaque references and refresh metadata.
- Central `shipment` invalidation covering shipment list/detail, nested containers and relevant linked/read/dashboard/badge query families; subscriptions on the SPA shipment list and detail.
- Immediate actor reconciliation from Shipment/document/container API responses, with an authorized refetch for legacy archive/restore responses that return no updated resource.
- Server-computed Shipment permissions/actions/version; status and metadata writes compare the optional `expected_version` under the shipment row lock and map stale versions to 409. Container updates compare both container and parent Shipment versions under locks. The detail preserves unsaved form state after a peer signal or 409 and pauses submissions until the user explicitly discards edits and reloads.
- Source dispositions for all 13 shipment/container/document mutation rows, event/payload inventory entries, cache-registry note, and one focused backend plus one invalidation-map regression test. Tests were authored but not run.

## Current slice — delta 320: Vehicle fleet and availability refresh

Source tracing confirmed that Vehicle writes came from fleet CRUD, AssetService asset association, DeliveryService status/confirmation/dispatch transitions, and DeliveryAttemptOutcomeService physical truck-return reconciliation. Source review also ruled out the previously recorded late-truck-recovery Vehicle write candidate: `DeliveryAttemptOutcomeService::receiveLate()` does not update Vehicle.

Added:

Update/archive/restore accept optional `expected_version` checked under row locks and return 409 for stale writes; the fleet UI submits versions, renders row actions from server data, preserves edit drafts on conflict, and asks the user to reopen refreshed data before retrying.

## Current slice — delta 342: realtime invalidation does not replay assignment planner writes

Source tracing confirmed that active WorkOrder/ProductionSchedule invalidation includes `['production','assignment-suggestion']`, whose generated mode called a POST that persists pending schedule rows. Failed POSTs can retry and new input keys still produce proposals. This keeps the existing interaction and API contract while preventing realtime pings from initiating repeated writes.

## Current slice — delta 359: move live analytics metrics from polling to event refresh

`useMetric` performed a 60-second network refetch for every `live` metric, despite complete current source paths for both registered live metrics. `ReturnRequest` and `WorkOrder` writes retire their independent `AnalyticsCache` generations in `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver`; the same models are observed by `BadgeInvalidationObserver`, which stages the after-commit `BadgesChanged` signal. Sidebar's shared `useBadges` subscriber receives it, and the central `badges_changed` invalidation map includes the `['dashboard']` prefix that covers metric query keys. RealtimeProvider already provides active-query catch-up when the channel is degraded. The client no longer sets a network `refetchInterval`; a one-shot local timer still marks a live metric stale after two minutes, without a network request. Hourly and daily stale thresholds are likewise display-only; monthly snapshots do not become stale from elapsed time.

The new focused `MetricPanel` regression first failed with three API calls over 120 seconds, then passed after the change: one initial API read, no timer-driven refetch over two minutes, and the existing stale badge appears at the freshness deadline (6/6 tests, 4.77 s). No two-user browser scenario, socket delivery, cache hit-rate, API-load, or p95 measurement was run. The polling inventory now has 20 rows: 8 active declarations, 1 unreferenced declaration, and 11 removed historical entries. The two current live metrics are source-mapped; future metric definitions still need the same event path before being classified as live.

## Current slice — delta 361: restart-safe scheduler and live cache/outbox audit

The live dev scheduler had exited after `schedule:run-fail-fast` returned non-zero for `ar:run-dunning`; its shell loop used `set -e` and its Compose service had no restart policy. Docker recorded the scheduler exited with code 1 and `restart=no` on 2026-10-29. That stopped scheduled cache audits/pruning and the scheduled outbox recovery dispatcher along with unrelated scheduled work. The exact business failure inside `ar:run-dunning` is not diagnosed here and is logged separately in `FINDINGS.md`.

Updated `.dev-runtime/docker-compose.override.yml` so a failed scheduler tick is logged and the loop continues after 60 seconds, and the scheduler restarts after an unexpected process exit. Compose configuration validation passed. Recreated only the scheduler service, without rebuilding or restarting the SPA/API/Reverb; the live scheduler is now running with `restart=unless-stopped`. Its startup tick ran the scheduled outbox dispatcher successfully. No business endpoint was invoked and no test suite was run. Compose reported an existing `ogami-migrate` orphan; it was left untouched.

The live API configuration resolves the cache store to the separate `redis-cache` service and broadcasts to Redis connection `broadcasts`, queue `broadcasts`, with the broadcast Redis logical database set to 1. The dedicated broadcasts Horizon supervisor is configured for one process and `horizon:status` reports running. A metadata-only `cache:audit --quiet-keys` inspected 185 active application-cache entries: zero lacked TTLs, zero exceeded 1 MiB, and none had unavailable size metadata. A bounded `cache:prune-unbounded --apply --limit=500` then found and deleted zero entries. These commands read no cache values. Source scan found all 116 broadcast event classes implement both `ShouldBroadcast` and `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, use the dedicated broadcasts trait, and none use `ShouldBroadcastNow`; the trait selects queue/connection but the explicit interface provides the commit boundary. This rules out the class-level before-commit broadcast hypothesis, not missing dispatches, channel authorization, delivery, UI refetch, or every mutation path.

The scheduler recovery removes the current operational cause for cache maintenance and outbox catch-up being stopped, but semantic freshness of every cached value is not established by TTL metadata. Runtime queue/WebSocket delivery, cache hit rate, old-generation key counts, two-user UI behavior, and p95 latency remain unverified. A cross-doc audit also corrected an obsolete no-restore-invalidation finding against `DatabaseRestoreCacheInvalidator` and its private SPA reload signal; restore coverage is source-wired but runtime unverified, and the explicit owner map can drift. No broad tests or suite ran for this delta.

## Current slice — delta 363: recover outbox dead letters and restore realtime health

The same two-context browser run showed `Reconnecting` while the manager socket authorized and received events. Read-only inspection found 35 failed `event_outbox` rows (19 AdminDashboardChanged, 10 ActivityFeedChanged, 6 AdminUserChanged), each at three attempts and carrying a `RuntimeException` category for an event type unregistered by the worker. The current codec supports all three types. Broadcast queues were empty, but `RealtimeQueueHealth` treats any failed outbox row as degraded, which held the global connection indicator in `Reconnecting` and enabled backoff catch-up. The old worker's in-memory codec was not captured; stale long-running worker code is strongly supported by the failure and lifecycle evidence.

Reviewed the failed types and confirmed that decode failed before dispatch, the events carry refresh metadata only, and replay cannot repeat business writes. Ran the supported `php artisan outbox:dispatch --retry-failed --limit=100`; it requeued and enqueued 35 messages. Runtime verification then found all 2,575 outbox rows published, zero affected failed rows, `high=0`, `broadcasts=0`, and `delivery_healthy=true`. No business records or cache keys were changed. `make dev-refresh-php` remains the local process refresh command; production deployment must terminate/restart Horizon after PHP changes. Updated Phase 0/1, FINDINGS, and cache/realtime docs with the evidence and limits.

Runtime inspection confirmed the dev stack runs separate healthy Redis containers: queue/broadcast on `redis` and application cache on `redis-cache`. API config resolves the queue connection to DB 2 and the cache store to DB 3; the dedicated broadcast queue uses DB 1 on the same non-evicting queue Redis instance. Runtime policies are `noeviction` for queue/broadcast Redis and `allkeys-lru` for the isolated cache Redis. This rules out cache LRU eviction of queued broadcasts in the observed dev topology; production topology was not inspected. Horizon source config has one local consumer on the dedicated `broadcasts` queue.

The metadata-only local audit inspected 211 cache entries: 0 without TTL, 0 above 1 MiB, and 0 with unavailable size metadata. The bounded prune dry-run found 0 candidates and deleted none. No cache values or key names were read/printed. `ogamiph.dev` returned HTTP 200. This confirms point-in-time key hygiene and site availability only; it does not prove semantic invalidation, queue delivery, two-user rendering, cache hit rate, or latency. No code changes or tests were run in this slice. The system-wide mutation/UI matrix and cache-freshness proof remain incomplete.

## Current slice — delta 404: trace PriceRequest lifecycle and expiry

Mapped nine reachable status edges: customer creation as Requested; direct internal proposal creation as PendingFinance; Requested→PendingFinance and Offered→PendingFinance when sales submits or revises terms; PendingFinance→Offered on Finance approval; Offered→Accepted/Rejected by the customer; open Requested/PendingFinance/Offered→Withdrawn by internal sales or finance; and PendingFinance/Offered→Expired by the daily pricing command. A re-save while already PendingFinance revises offer/version data without changing status. The daily `pricing:review-agreements --dispatch-only` invocation only dispatches BOM review outbox rows and does not expire PriceRequests.

## Current slice — delta 461: audit shared cache-generation failure boundaries

Reviewed the shared `CacheGeneration` and `FailOpenCache` helpers and representative high-impact cache families: dashboard/widget snapshots, procurement summaries, inventory/production summaries, role permissions, DSS parameters, payroll statutory tables/progress, settings and holiday data. On the normal write path, generation keys are namespaced, initialized atomically with non-reusable UUIDs, and the covered business writers request rotation after commit; payload readers use bounded TTLs and cache read/write exceptions fall back to source data. Authenticated API responses receive `private, no-store` plus `Vary: Cookie, Authorization`; the service worker bypasses API/Vite requests, uses network-first navigation, and clears versioned old app caches on activation/logout.

This source audit did not reproduce a stale result or identify a normal-path invalidation defect, so no product code changed. One residual risk remains evidenced in the implementation and cache registry: if an after-commit generation-marker write fails while Redis still serves reads, some families log the failure but readers can continue to address the previous payload namespace until that payload's TTL expires (30 seconds to one hour across the reviewed families). The durable `event_outbox` cannot currently retry cache invalidation generically: its codec/dispatcher decode and publish typed events, while generation rotation is registered in separate best-effort callbacks and carries no cache-family descriptor. The cache audit only verifies TTL/size metadata; no live Redis invalidation-failure injection, cache hit-rate, or API-latency measurement ran. The next cache slice should either add durable retry/correctness evidence for this failure boundary or source-trace a specific unverified writer; do not treat bounded TTL as proof of immediate invalidation.

## Current slice — delta 463: conflict-safe Recruitment stage actions

The stale Recruitment action path was reproduced without touching live data. A PHPUnit regression set the stored application stage to `screening` and submitted an updated-client request with `expected_stage=new`; before the fix it returned HTTP 422 with interview-required validation errors rather than a state conflict. A React component regression mocked that 409 boundary and, before the fix, the detail did not fetch the current application and kept the old “Move to Screening” button and confirmation open.

The stage endpoint now accepts optional `expected_stage`, validates conditional interview fields against that stage, and compares it to the current stage while holding the application row lock. A mismatch returns HTTP 409 with code `recruitment_state_conflict`; no state write or candidate mail occurs. Updated SPA advance/reject/interview requests send the rendered stage. Conflict handling invalidates the application detail/list and linked posting query families; the basic confirmation closes, and a typed interview draft remains intact with the server message while the detail refreshes. Legacy clients that omit `expected_stage` remain accepted and retain the previous server-side state revalidation path.

Verification: red-first API case then focused API checks passed (3 tests / 13 assertions, including stale 409, legacy request compatibility, and existing hire-permission denial). The Recruitment detail component suite passes 9/9, including stale-action removal and interview-draft preservation. Scoped ESLint passes for the changed API/page/test TS files. PHP syntax checks and Pint pass for the changed backend files; `git diff --check` passes. No live Recruitment two-context browser run was attempted: the running Compose stack is production-configured (`APP_ENV=production`, database `ogami`, SMTP mailer), and a real stage action can notify an actual candidate. Existing two-context Purchase Request evidence remains in the Phase 0 notes; it does not prove the Recruitment flow or the Recruitment SLO.

A project-wide SPA `npm run typecheck` surfaced independent existing diagnostics after about two minutes (budgeting detail callback type, Recruitment posting detail missing `JobPosting` type, two unused HR-training test imports, recent-items test arity, and Vite proxy config typing); it exited non-zero. ## Current slice — delta 464: retire cache generations when marker writes fail

The delta 461 source audit identified a freshness gap: if a post-commit marker `put` returned false or threw while the cache remained readable, several owners left the old marker in place. A later authorized API refetch could still select the old versioned payload. Added shared `CacheGeneration::rotateDefault()` over the core `rotate()` helper: on write failure it attempts to delete the prior marker and checks for absence if the store returns false from `forget()`. A later `current()` call atomically creates a fresh UUID namespace. Migrated dashboard and scoped widget generations, badges, analytics, financial statements, holidays, role permissions, settings, inventory and production dashboards, DSS parameter generations, government contribution tables/imports, payroll progress, and procurement summaries.

Red-first `DashboardCacheGenerationFailureTest` reproduced the stale snapshot with a rejected marker write; after the fix it passes (1 test, 5 assertions) and returns the fresh source value. PHP syntax checks passed for the 16 changed PHP/test files. A 13-file targeted PHPUnit run completed 29 tests / 118 assertions, with one error and two failures unrelated to the cache helper: `StatementCacheAfterCommitTest` calls a nonexistent `JournalEntryObserver::saved()` method; `GovernmentTableRealtimeTest` expects one outbox row but the isolated two-test file also finds two. Those verification defects remain in `FINDINGS.md`; no unrelated behavior was changed. No full suite ran. No live Redis dual-failure injection, two-user screen, cache hit-rate, API p95, or latency measurement ran. If marker write and marker retirement both fail while old marker reads remain available, stale snapshots remain a residual risk until their owner TTL expires; the helper logs that outcome at error level. Retired payload keys remain TTL-bounded rather than being synchronously enumerated/deleted.

## Current slice — delta 465: Recruitment server actions and posting version conflicts

The delta 462/463 source review left a concrete action-state gap: Recruitment resources omitted viewer-specific actions, permissions and versions, so the posting detail rendered Edit/Publish/Close/Archive/Restore from local permission and status values. A red-first API test observed `permissions: null`; a red-first posting-detail test showed the stale Publish control still rendered when the API advertised only Edit.

Added `RecruitmentApplicationActions` and `RecruitmentPostingActions` projections. Application and posting resources now expose a per-viewer permission object, `available_actions`, and a version derived from `updated_at`. Posting archive availability checks the application count; the posting index and application list preload counts so resource projection does not issue a count query per list row. Posting detail controls now render from the server action list.

Posting update, status, archive and restore requests accept an optional `expected_version`. Services compare it against the row-locked version and controllers return the typed `recruitment_state_conflict` HTTP 409 if it is stale. Application arrival can change archive eligibility without changing the posting's own `updated_at`; the locked archive path also maps that relation change to 409 for version-aware clients, while legacy calls without a version retain the prior 422 business-rule response. Older clients that omit the field remain compatible. The edit form and posting detail send their rendered version. Detail conflict handling refreshes the posting, list and linked application query families; update conflict handling preserves typed form values, fetches current posting metadata directly through the authorized API, and offers a review action before the user reopens the latest posting.

The actor applies the status response directly to the detail query and invalidates related lists. A component test exposed that invalidating the exact detail query immediately after writing the response could replace the response with an older refetch; that exact-key invalidation was removed. Posting-list and linked-application invalidations remain.

## Current slice — delta 476: permission-gated realtime and cache operations health

The existing Settings system-information endpoint reported framework versions and drivers, while the authenticated `/api/v1/realtime/health` endpoint exposed only a content-free healthy/degraded bit. Operators could not see queue/outbox backlog, failed outbox count, Reverb listener reachability, cache Redis memory headroom, or when the scheduled cache audit last completed. Added `GET /api/v1/admin/system-health`, guarded by the existing `admin.settings.manage` permission, and a 30-second-refresh panel in Settings → System Info. It reports only aggregate counts/status/memory, never Redis hostnames, credentials, cache keys/values or record data. Reverb is explicitly labeled a TCP reachability probe; it does not prove WebSocket handshake, channel authorization or client delivery. The UI is shown only to settings administrators.

`RealtimeQueueHealth` now returns the one-second cached aggregate snapshot used by both the existing authenticated realtime health bit and the admin view. The cache-audit command stores a TTL-bounded (8-day) aggregate summary at `ops:cache-audit:last:v1`; audit completion is not failed if this optional summary write fails, and the panel displays Unknown. The cache registry and source-call ledger now include both entries. Reindexed the cache-call source ledger after the broad module edits: 384 source-line rows now match their current PHP lines/snippets exactly (120 app, 207 tests, 57 migrations/seeders). Versioned payload data, business rules and mutation behavior did not change.

Red/green evidence: the new feature test first failed with a missing route, then passed with the permission-gated response shape and `private, no-store` headers (1 test, 26 assertions); the API cache-audit regression first failed because no summary was saved, then passed (2 tests, 13 assertions); existing realtime queue-health regressions pass after matching the new aggregate queue read (4 tests, 14 assertions). The focused panel tests pass (2 tests). PHP syntax checks passed for the changed backend files. `npm run typecheck` completed with six existing errors in budgeting detail, HR recruitment/training test fixtures, the recent-items test fixture and Vite proxy typing; it reported no error in the new panel/API files. A local service snapshot and two metadata-only cache audits recorded healthy delivery, Reverb TCP reachable, ~1.49/96 MiB cache memory, 264 keys with no TTL/size issues, and zero dry-run prune candidates. `ogamiph.dev` returned the SPA and Vite module routes with HTTP 200; Vite/Nginx logs show the new panel module served through HMR. No authenticated human UI session, WebSocket handshake/client delivery, queue stop/restart, cache hit-rate, API latency or p95 measurement ran. See [`07-operations-health.md`](07-operations-health.md). At delta 476 this panel was operational visibility only; delta 478 subsequently isolated generation markers from the evictable payload store. The panel still does not complete Phase 7/8.

## Current slice — delta 478: isolate cache-generation markers from evictable payloads

Confirmed a broad cache design risk in `api/config/cache.php`, `api/config/database.php`, and the local Redis topology: production generation markers had shared the disposable `redis-cache` store configured with `allkeys-lru`. If both marker rotation attempts and fallback retirement fail while the prior marker remains readable, subsequent requests can keep selecting its older payload namespace. This is a verified source/config failure mode, but it was not independently reproduced by live Redis eviction/failure and is not attributed to the 3.508 s PR peer-render sample.

Added a named `generations` Laravel cache store and Redis connection (persistent `REDIS_HOST`, logical DB 4 by default) and changed every direct production `CacheGeneration::current(Cache::store(), ...)` caller to `currentDefault()`. Existing `rotateDefault()` now resolves that same marker store. Payload caches remain on their prior store and preserve their TTLs. PHPUnit uses an isolated array-backed marker store. `cache:audit` and `cache:prune-unbounded` now scan both payload and generation stores, label keys with their store, and only prune an explicitly scanned unbounded key in that same store. Existing legacy marker/snapshot keys were not flushed; they are unreachable after namespace cutover and expire under their existing bounded TTLs.

Red/green evidence: the new `CacheGenerationIsolationTest` first failed with missing `currentDefault()` and missing Redis connection/store configuration. The focused serial run then passed **11 tests / 55 assertions** across generation isolation, marker write retry/retirement, Redis metadata scan/delete, and cache audit/prune command behavior. PHPUnit reported warnings because `/var/www/.env` is absent in the container; assertions passed. After regeneration, the cache-source ledger has **427 unique rows** (131 application, 239 tests, 57 migrations/seeders), with every recorded line/snippet matching its source. After restarting API, Horizon and scheduler, a metadata-only audit inspected **441** keys: 70 generation-store markers and 371 payload-store entries, all with TTLs and sizes available, none over 1 MiB. The safe prune dry-run found 0 unbounded keys and deleted 0. All three restarted services are running; `https://ogamiph.dev/` and `/@vite/client` returned HTTP 200. No full suite, frontend test, or browser E2E ran in this slice; no live Redis eviction was induced. Cache was not disabled and no cache-hit/API-p95/database-load change was measured.

## Current slice — delta 482: reduce duplicate realtime catch-up and remeasure final approval

Source tracing found that initial Echo connection and the first degraded health probe each launched a provider-wide invalidation while active page subscriptions already performed a narrower authorized catch-up on channel acknowledgement. This duplicated API reads during page mount. Focused tests reproduced both extra invalidations before the change. `RealtimeProvider` now skips the initial global catch-up, reserves it for a true reconnect or later delivery-health transition, and relies on each active private channel's onSubscribed callback for its initial scoped refresh. An initial degraded health result now enters the existing visibility-aware active-query fallback rather than issuing a second immediate global pass. The visible delivery-health probe cadence changed from two to ten seconds; its timeout remains two seconds. This lowers request frequency but can delay detection of a stalled broadcast worker by up to one health interval, so degraded-detection/request-volume tradeoffs still need measurement.

Focused checks passed serially: `provider.test.tsx` 7/7, `invalidationMap.test.ts` 76/76, and `purchase-requests.test.ts` 1/1. ESLint passed for the changed provider, central invalidation, Purchase Request API/page and E2E files. One headless Chromium four-context final-approval run passed in 39.9 s: Finance created/advanced, System Administrator viewed, and VP approved with the actor socket disabled. Actor state/buttons reconciled from PATCH 200 within the 2 s assertion. The peer received three private `chain.refresh` frames, refetched the authorized record, and rendered Approved/removing Cancel 1.726 s after the API response; its post-action GET took 1.452 s. The response was `no-store, private`, Cloudflare marked it DYNAMIC, and it did not come from a service worker. Health returned healthy twice about ten seconds apart. This one response-to-render sample is not a commit-to-UI p95.

The latest run recorded three successful peer detail responses plus one aborted stale request, versus eight successful responses in an earlier scenario run; this comparison is observational. Earlier final-approval samples included 3.508 s and a 3.628 s SLO-bound failure, so the route's detail/action matrix cells remain PARTIAL. API logs still reported the PHP-FPM pool reaching its configured maximum of eight workers at 17:46:48, 17:46:57 and 17:47:10 UTC. The upstream default five-worker cap was raised to eight in both dev and production Dockerfiles, but it is not raised again without a per-child memory profile on the intended 4 GB target. The latest API request improved, but saturation warnings continue; attribution of each latency to FPM is not isolated. No full suite, API p95, cache-hit rate, or per-child memory benchmark was collected.

## Delta 485 — prioritize live detail refresh and release successful form redirects (2026-11-02 UTC)

The four-context final-approval scenario first reproduced two frontend problems. In the first post-guard run, Purchase Request POST returned 201 and submit returned 200, and the database showed a submitted request, but the creator stayed on the create form with the unsaved-changes confirmation open; the test timed out before the other roles advanced. `useFormSafety` kept the guard active from `formState.isDirty && !mutation.isSuccess`, while TanStack Query invokes a mutation's `onSuccess` before setting `isSuccess`. The callback's successful `navigate()` therefore hit the active guard. Added a one-shot post-save navigation release to the shared guard and `useFormSafety`, then wired all 50 success-callback redirects across 47 form pages using that same API. Cancel links retain the guard.

A later run completed the role flow but missed the 2 s peer-render target at 2.998 s. The trace showed three `/dashboards/badges` reads starting 57–75 ms after the actor response and taking 2.09–2.87 s; the three peer detail reads started about 182 ms after the response and took 1.62–1.98 s. API logs again showed the PHP-FPM pool reaching its dev cap of ten. Source tracing confirmed record detail, lists, related records, counts and dashboards were scheduled together through one invalidation lane. The central map now coalesces live detail/additional record keys for 100 ms, then schedules list/related/count/dashboard families and shared signals in the near-live lane at 300 ms. This preserves caching and debouncing while giving the detail/action refetch a head start.

Red/green evidence: the save-navigation guard regression failed before implementation because no navigation-release callback existed, then the focused guard file passed 3/3. The new invalidation-order regression failed before the scheduler split because the badge and detail queries started together; after the change, the focused invalidation map file passed 77/77. No broad API suite or full SPA suite ran. The attempted project typecheck exited 143 without diagnostics; its orphaned worker was stopped. The prior recorded project typecheck has unrelated existing errors, so this slice has no clean project-wide typecheck result.

The final four-context headless Chromium scenario passed with Purchasing creator, Finance intermediate approver, System Administrator peer detail, and VP final approver. VP's WebSocket was closed. Its PATCH returned 200 / Approved and the actor rendered Approved without the socket. The peer channel delivered `chain.refresh`; the authorized detail GET began 166 ms after the actor response, returned Approved with `actions.can_cancel=false` in 911 ms, and the peer rendered Approved in 1.359 s. Cancel disappeared in 1.097 s. Two chain refresh frames were observed at +31 ms and +901 ms. Source and the durable outbox ledger show that final PR approval can be followed by two separate conversion-state writes: `ConsolidatePurchaseOrders` marks conversion pending, then marks it manual_required when no line can be sourced; each write stages a post-commit `ChainStepAdvanced`. The latest tagged approved test request ended in `manual_required`, and its ledger contained three approved-state chain refresh messages, all published. The browser run observed two frames but did not capture event IDs or wait for the final conversion state, so it neither proves duplicate delivery nor verifies receipt of every later signal. Response headers were `max-age=0, must-revalidate, no-store, private`, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, and `fromServiceWorker=false`, so this sample did not use a browser, service-worker, or shared-proxy cached detail response.

This is one passing timing sample after a 2.998 s miss and older 3.508/3.628 s misses, not a p95 or stable SLO. PHP-FPM warnings at the 10-child cap continued in adjacent logs; their contribution to the successful sample was not isolated. Matrix detail/action coverage remains `PARTIAL`. Lists, linked records, badges, dashboards, and approval-inbox results for final approval remain unverified. No API p95, cache hit rate, or cache performance impact was measured. The successful test-created approved request remains as local test data. The single abandoned pending request from the failed creator attempt was cancelled through `PurchaseRequestService` using its requester; previously approved tagged test rows were left untouched. A post-fix AST audit confirmed exactly one navigation allowance before each of the 50 `useFormSafety` success redirects; it also caught and removed one duplicate allowance in the already-tested Purchase Request creator. A separate scan inventoried 12 direct guard files and found no protected mutation-success redirect outside that set.


## Delta 486 — reconcile inbound Shipment source notes with registered realtime path (2026-11-02 UTC)

A source audit found the matrix still claiming that no realtime source existed for 13 inbound Shipment/Container/document/landed-cost mutations. Current source registers `ShipmentRealtimeObserver` for Shipment and `ShipmentComponentRealtimeObserver` for Container, ShipmentDocument, and ShipmentLandedCost. The child observer resolves the parent Shipment and calls `ShipmentRealtimeService`; `OutboxService::recordCoalesced` stages one content-free `ShipmentChanged` per PostgreSQL transaction, published after commit. The event targets private `shipment.{hashId}` and `entity.shipment.visible` channels; both callbacks require an active user, enabled Supply Chain module, and the same shipment-view permission used by read routes. The SPA event map invalidates Shipment detail/list, nested containers, linked GRN/PO/Bill, dashboard, KPI and badge query families. No missing Shipment producer was found, so no application code changed.

A second source comparison corrected 11 more stale event-absence traces: DeliverySchedule create/decision routes reach the registered observer; PriceRequest offer/respond reach PriceRequestRealtimeObserver; SalesOrderAgreement updates refresh the parent order and mapped agreement keys; SalesOrder fulfillment changes alter the MRP fingerprint and reach the existing SalesOrder refresh path; KPI compute stages KpiSnapshotChanged; Vehicle restore reaches VehicleRealtimeObserver; due-calibration saves reach QualityRecordRealtimeObserver; and master-data imports reach model or importer batch refreshes. Supplier-owned legacy schedule actions remain HTTP 410 and therefore do not mutate a record. These are source dispositions, not live delivery results. The 24 updated matrix source notes retain `NOT_VERIFIED` UI outcomes where no user scenario exists.

The exact `ShipmentRealtimeTest.php` file passed **2 tests / 8 assertions** in 19.18 seconds. It confirms parent/child writes coalesce to one content-free outbox entry and stale parent shipment versions are rejected. This is focused feature-test evidence only; it does not prove live Reverb delivery, role-to-role UI refresh, or every mutation's behavior. No broad suite, browser scenario, latency or cache-hit measurement ran.


## Delta 487 — same-user dashboard layout refresh and scoped query cache (2026-11-02 UTC)

The dashboard layout path had no event for save/reset, leaving sibling tabs with stale layout and version state. The empty-widget save path deleted existing rows then returned before any refresh hook. A component reproduction also showed that an incoming layout update overwrote the picker draft while it was open. Focused API and component tests captured all three cases before their fixes.

`DashboardLayoutChanged` now stages one content-free outbox refresh per user and transaction when canonical user-owned rows change. It is broadcast after commit to the existing private exact-self user preferences channel; other accounts cannot subscribe, and the wire payload has no user hash or layout contents. AppLayout maps it through the shared realtime hook to personal dashboard layout and widget-data invalidation. Save/reset responses seed the acting user's layout cache immediately. Dashboard widget catalog, layout and widget-data keys now carry `realtimeIdentityKey` (account, role, employee, permissions, delegation and feature scope). The picker retains unsaved arrangement changes when a remote version arrives, alerts the user, and blocks submission of the stale version until it is reopened against current data. No Laravel layout cache exists; no cache was disabled or removed.

Focused verification passed serially: DashboardLayout API regressions 4 tests / 14 assertions; broadcast contract/inventory guards 2 tests / 1,221 assertions; private preferences-channel owner/foreign-user authorization 1 test / 3 assertions; DashboardPicker and realtime invalidation SPA files 84 tests, including same-user permission-scope key separation. Targeted frontend ESLint and Pint `--test` passed. Pint initially found three style issues in touched PHP files; formatting was applied only to those files. No live two-tab Reverb delivery, peer render timing, cache-hit rate or performance benchmark was run. No full API/project suite ran.

## Delta 489 — preserve mutation responses and cancel stale reads across mapped query families (2026-11-02 UTC)

A red-first React Query regression reproduced an older pending detail request overwriting a fresh mutation response after the detail key was excluded from family invalidation. The same source pattern was found in mutation handlers across Assets, Quality, Inventory, Production, Recruitment, Accounting, B2B customer portal, Supply Chain, Maintenance, Warehouse and dashboard layout flows. A source scan initially found 50 candidate set/invalidate pairs; manual review separated exact detail races from independent lists, partial child updates and intentionally recomputed pages. The stale-risk matrix now annotates 72 route rows whose frontend consumer uses the repaired shared invalidation path. All actor/peer UI outcome cells remain unchanged.

The central `invalidatePrefixesOnce()` now cancels older in-flight reads for both preserved exact keys and affected active prefixes with `revert:false`. `invalidateRealtimeChange()` and `invalidateRealtimeSignal()` accept exact-key exclusions for API resources already installed in the actor's cache, while still invalidating other mapped lists, linked records, badges, inboxes and dashboards. `invalidateQueryPrefixesExcept()` routes generic family invalidation through this same implementation for custom query families. Partial child responses do not exclude the parent query, so its current API representation is refetched. Realtime peer invalidations continue through the same map without exclusions. No backend cache was removed, no API response caching was disabled, and no domain rules changed.

Verification: red/green stale in-flight query regression passes; the generic query invalidation, central invalidation map and shared status-action hook files pass **83 SPA tests**. Targeted ESLint passes across changed files. An isolated Work Order failed-receipt retry test passes after limiting its error refresh to the exact detail/output families. A multi-file UI run reported 151 passing tests and one Asset association timeout; the same Asset test passed in isolation. A separate Work Order detail-file run produced Pusher-constructor errors and failures outside the isolated retry scenario, so that file is not claimed green. No full API suite ran. No browser two-user case, live Reverb delivery, latency, cache hit rate, or API p95 was measured; the 72 matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED` for runtime UI outcomes.

## Delta 492 — preserve a clean query state when cancelling stale reads (2026-11-02 UTC)

The GRN actor regression exposed a cancellation side effect in the shared in-flight guard: `revert:false` stopped an old request but left the TanStack query in `error`, so the detail page showed “Failed to load GRN” after success. ## Delta 493 — fan out attendance-driven crew refreshes to production list viewers (2026-11-02 UTC)

The source path was traced end to end. `AttendanceRealtimeService::stage()` resolves active work orders whose crew includes the changed employee. `AttendanceChanged` already sent a minimal event to `chain.work_order.{id}`, and detail hooks listened there, but production list hooks subscribe to `entity.work_order.visible`; they did not subscribe to the exact per-record channels. Production viewers also need not have `attendance.view`, so they cannot rely on `entity.attendance.visible`. Before the change, an affected production list viewer could miss the crew/readiness refresh even while a linked detail viewer received it.

When at least one active related work order exists, `AttendanceChanged` now also targets the private `entity.work_order.visible` channel. That callback requires the active user and both `production.view` and `production.work_orders.view`; the payload remains content-free and contains no work-order IDs. `useRealtimeList('work_order')` listens for `.attendance.changed`, and the central work-order invalidation map now includes work-order lists plus crew, crew options, readiness, and assignment/reschedule suggestion query families. Recipients still refetch through their own authorized API scope.

Red/green evidence: the list-hook regression failed before the listener existed and passes after; the event-contract regression failed before the list channel was added and passes after. `BroadcastEventContractTest.php` passes 37 tests / 1,349 assertions; the work-order channel authorization case passes 1 test / 2 assertions; the focused SPA hook and invalidation files pass 120 tests. ESLint and Pint pass. The first combined SPA run exposed two assertions that assumed an immediate or first-call invalidation; they now wait for the mapped family in the near-live lane, after which both files pass. This is source and test-harness reproduction, not a two-user browser reproduction: live attendance mutation, Reverb delivery, list rendering and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No broad API suite ran.


## Delta 494 — route private realtime refreshes through the shared invalidator (2026-11-02 UTC)

Those paths could make multiple independent query-cache traversals for one ping and could leave an older active read to race its follow-up refresh.

Realtime hooks now route scoped prefixes through the same TanStack key matching, normal-revert cancellation, active authorized refetch and 100 ms coalescing used by record events. ## Delta 496 — Leave actions and stale-decision recovery (2026-11-02 UTC)

Leave detail, list, create and batch handlers route refresh through the central invalidation map, with the detail hook retaining successful actor API state. Explicit action arrays control the UI; the previous `can_approve` fallback remains for old responses during rolling deployments.

No live Leave mutation or two-user scenario ran; this delta does not prove Reverb delivery or peer action-button rendering. API tests used the test database only. ## Delta 581 — service-worker cache policy at the Cloudflare edge (2026-11-03 UTC)

Reproduced a public-edge cache-policy mismatch: before the change, local TLS Nginx and Vite served `/sw.js` as `no-cache`, but `https://ogamiph.dev/sw.js` returned `Cache-Control: max-age=14400` with `cf-cache-status: REVALIDATED`. The active ignored `.dev-runtime/nginx.conf` had only the generic SPA proxy; it had no exact service-worker location. Added an exact `/sw.js` route to the active VPS override and tracked dev Nginx template; it sets `Cache-Control: no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0`, `CDN-Cache-Control: no-store`, and `Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control: no-store`, while re-including the standard security headers. Production Nginx's exact worker route now uses the same policy. After `nginx -t` and graceful reload, origin returned the new headers and the public host returned `no-store` with `cf-cache-status: BYPASS` on two consecutive requests. The app shell and five sampled Vite endpoints (`/src/main.tsx`, `/@react-refresh`, `/@vite/client`, `/src/lib/echo.ts`, `/src/hooks/useRealtimeEvent.ts`) remain HTTP 200/no-cache with Cloudflare `DYNAMIC`; an unauthenticated auth-user API response remains 401/private/no-store. Five serial requests measured the 3,533-byte script at 6.8–7.5 ms from the local TLS origin and 56.9–283.3 ms through the public edge; this is a small worker-update fetch, not an app-page latency or a cache-hit comparison. No Cloudflare API credential was available to purge any previously stored edge object; normal requests now bypass it, and it will expire under its prior TTL. No API payload, business row or Redis cache entry was changed. No record-level UI symptom was reproduced in this slice.

## Delta 614 — fix Inspection peer-detail refetch race and verify cancellation live (2026-11-03 UTC)

A three-context Playwright scenario on `https://ogamiph.dev` reproduced the reported cross-role stale Inspection detail after the event and authorized GET returned the committed cancellation. ## Delta 622 — protect the Laravel queue restart marker from LRU eviction and cleanup (2026-11-03 UTC)

The no-TTL entry was not an orphaned record cache: it was Laravel's worker restart signal. Source tracing explained its recurrence: Horizon's `horizon:terminate` writes `illuminate:queue:restart` forever, while the previous listener only handled `queue:restart`. Under `allkeys-lru`, cache pressure could evict this control key, and `cache:prune-unbounded --apply` could delete it. Added a single exact store/key allowlist, made the command audit report that persistent infrastructure marker separately, protected it in the atomic prune reader and command, retained it after both restart commands, and changed dev/prod Compose cache eviction to `volatile-lru`. Recreating the dev Redis cache container applied the durable policy and cleared its disposable cache keyspace; no database/business data changed, but temporary cold-cache impact was not measured. After startup, audit inspected 449 entries: zero unbounded application entries, one expected persistent infrastructure key, zero oversized/unknown-size entries; prune apply found/deleted zero. The marker was present with TTL `-1`; the exact command that recreated it after the Redis restart was not captured. Focused cache/restart tests pass 7 tests / 34 assertions, with seven environment warnings; `docker compose config -q` passes. Outbox table sample: 11,911 rows, maximum stored JSON 645 bytes, p99 372 bytes. No full suite or browser scenario ran. Current stale matrix UI cells, cache hit-rate, API p95, production topology and forced-pressure behavior remain incomplete.

## Delta 631 — measure final-approval freshness in the browser (2026-11-03 UTC)

One serial real-Chromium scenario passed in 39.1 seconds against `ogamiph.dev`, reusing synthetic PR-202611-0040 after its Finance step. VP finalized it with the actor socket closed; the actor rendered the PATCH 200 response. An already-open System Administrator detail received three private `chain.refresh` frames at -30/+97/+466 ms from the actor response. The browser trace recorded one overlapping detail GET started +67 ms and canceled +201 ms, then one successful authorized detail GET started +197 ms; its response showed `approved` and `actions.can_cancel=false` at +1,614 ms, and the page MutationObserver saw Cancel disappear at +1,650 ms. This cancellation is intentional central invalidation behavior to prevent a possibly pre-commit/in-flight response from winning. The log ended after successful render, so it does not exclude a later queued catch-up read. Playwright observed the Purchasing creator list show Approved by +2,142 ms; two network-served list reads for that PR returned Approved. The list timing is a runner-observed upper bound, not a DOM timestamp. A read-only outbox check found three distinct ChainStepAdvanced rows at final approval/conversion time, each published once; the browser instrumentation did not capture event IDs, so they are not mapped one-to-one. Client invalidation coalesced the burst to one successful detail response in the observed window; one overlapping GET was canceled, and a later queued catch-up outside the log window is not ruled out. The route row is now PARTIAL for detail/actions (earlier variable samples remain) and PASS for this list outcome. The explicit transition row is PARTIAL; badges, dashboards, linked records and inbox remain unverified.

Both response classes had `no-store, private`; Cloudflare reported `DYNAMIC`, Age was absent, and serviceWorker=false. No cache data changed. The earlier 2,531 ms assertion used Playwright locator completion time; the same run's in-page observer measured 1,873 ms. The E2E now uses the observer timestamp for its 2 s detail/action assertion and anchors statuses to the PageHeader. Targeted ESLint and E2E TypeScript checks pass; the one E2E scenario passes. No full suite ran. One timed-out create-based attempt did not issue its create request; see `FINDINGS.md` for the separate source-confirmed UI guard candidate. This slice moved only synthetic dev PRs: PR-202611-0040 and an earlier synthetic pending test record were finalized; the diagnostic create runs produced synthetic PR-202611-0043 and PR-202611-0044. No real business PR was used.

## Delta 632 — explain the canceled Purchase Request detail read (2026-11-03 UTC)

Source inspection of `invalidatePrefixesOnce()` and its existing focused tests confirms the +67 ms GET canceled at +201 ms was deliberately superseded after a record signal; a fresh authorized GET started at +197 ms and returned Approved. The test logs separately capture successful responses and failed requests, so “one detail read” overstated the number of network attempts. The canceled request is not evidence of a missed event or stale cache. The invalidator also queues another pass when signals arrive during an active refetch, which can be needed if a later committed change races that read. This E2E log closed at the successful UI assertion and does not establish whether such a trailing pass occurred; no optimization change is justified without measuring that window. No production code changed and no tests were run.

## Delta 680 — version Overtime status mutations (2026-11-03 UTC)

The session was created and started through the API; both users opened the same Stock Count detail page. Warehouse Staff recorded a line through the UI. The count endpoint returned HTTP 200, the peer received a private Stock Count refresh and refetched the session through its own authorized API request, and both pages showed the line as Counted with Recount replacing Count without a reload. The single observed actor-response-to-peer-render sample was 1,058 ms. It is not a p95 or a commit-to-UI measurement. The test completed in 22.7 seconds, and its synthetic session was canceled by cleanup.

The browser trace also exposed a client race: `sessionUnavailable` includes React Query's `isFetching`; a detail refetch that began after the Count form submit was dispatched caused the submit callback to return without calling the API, leaving the dialog open. Stock Count service already locks and revalidates current state, so the form no longer silently drops an already-submitted request during that refetch. The button remains disabled while data is known to be stale; any submitted request reaches the server and receives its normal success/conflict result. The red-first component test failed because the mutation was not called while a refetch was pending. The focused component file passes 2/2 after the fix; its mocked realtime hooks verify actor row reconciliation from the API response independently of Echo.

Only the StockCountItem `pending|counted|verified → counted` route row promotes actor screen, peer detail, and per-item Count/Recount action cells. The merged page's session list, variance approval, related records, badges, dashboards, approval inbox, and socket-disabled browser behavior remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Cache metadata, values, policies and service settings were unchanged. No full API suite or project typecheck ran.

## Delta 702 — make external Payroll recompute and its refresh atomic (2026-11-03 UTC)

The recompute controller previously called `PayrollCalculatorService::computeForEmployee()`, which committed the replacement Payroll row plus related loan/adjustment/accrual writes, then staged `PayrollPeriodChanged(related_updated)` afterward. ## Delta 722 — reduce healthy delivery probes and retest cross-role Purchase Request approval (2026-11-03 UTC)

The four-context `purchase-request-final-approval-realtime.spec.ts` rerun against `https://ogamiph.dev` passed 1/1 in 53 seconds. Purchasing, Finance, System Administrator and VP contexts participated; the VP actor socket was disabled. The actor rendered the API's `approved` result. The peer detail channel received three `chain.refresh` frames; its authorized GET began 272 ms after the actor response and returned Approved with `actions.can_cancel=false` in 1,392 ms. The peer rendered Approved and removed Cancel at 1,620 ms; the Purchasing list rendered Approved at 2,220 ms, both without reload. The detail response was private/no-store, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, had no `Age` header and did not come from a service worker. The query listener saw 25 SQL queries totaling 74.3 ms across user/record lookup, eager loading, action projection and the snapshot; seven shared one normalized SQL fingerprint. The profile bypassed PHP-FPM, route middleware and network timing, so it does not isolate the slow browser GET. It does not justify a query or FPM configuration change by itself.

## Delta 724 — raise dev FPM concurrency after measured saturation (2026-11-03 UTC)

A focused four-context Purchase Request approval rerun initially rendered peer detail at 2.259 s and failed the 2.0 s assertion; another pre-change run timed out before the approval action while trying to load the peer's initial Pending detail. One post-change four-context E2E passed 1/1 in 49.4 s: the actor socket was closed, the actor rendered the API response, the peer received three private refresh frames and rendered Approved/removed Cancel at 1.821 s, and the list rendered Approved without reload at 2.694 s. The authorized detail response remained private/no-store, Cloudflare DYNAMIC, with no service-worker response. The run's detail GET browser duration was 1.988 s; other detail requests in the same log window still reached 4.545 s. There were no max-child warnings in the post-change log window. This is one uncontrolled post-change sample, not proof of causality or a stable SLO; p95 and memory peak under sustained load remain unmeasured. The pre-change failing scenario and final passing scenario both remain in Playwright artifacts. Route matrix outcome counts stay unchanged. No broad API suite ran.

## Delta 758 — return server-authoritative NCR cancellation eligibility (2026-11-04 UTC)

The NCR detail page rendered Cancel for any nonterminal record when the client permission snapshot allowed NCR management. `NcrService::cancel()` has additional locked guards: late incoming NCRs and NCRs with a settled MRB decision cannot be cancelled. ## Delta 902 — normalize every Reverb/Pusher refresh payload centrally (2026-11-05 UTC)

A source-shape audit found only 33 of 128 broadcast classes directly emitted all nine standard refresh fields. Added a `StandardizedPusherBroadcaster` decorator registered for both the configured `reverb` and fallback `pusher` drivers. It completes absent fields at the shared transport boundary, preserves legacy keys/event names/private channels, promotes only string opaque IDs into entity/actor/related metadata, and filters batched entity ID lists. Delta 903 corrected the Reverb-driver size guard to use server `max_request_size` (the installed server applies it to the complete buffered Pusher HTTP request), estimating the event body plus a 1 KiB HTTP overhead reserve. The Pusher fallback gets the common envelope without the Reverb-specific limit. The 128-row payload audit distinguishes source shape from the common wire envelope. Red-first focused checks failed before the normalizer and batch-ID guard existed; the focused test passes 6 cases / 15 assertions, with six PHPUnit warnings from the missing `/var/www/.env` in the container. Reverb configuration check passes, and the Horizon container was gracefully terminated and restarted so its workers reload the broadcaster; `horizon:status` reports running. No full suite, browser frame, multi-user scenario, cache mutation, or matrix promotion occurred. The transport contract is verified; delivery and UI freshness remain unverified.
